| description | With artillery's growing importance on the battlefield, the number of firearms kept increasing. But because each gun was made separately, it also required great effort to resupply. ### Canal de Castilla

| name | cas\_canal\_de\_castilla |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Canal de Castilla |
| description | Inspired by other water systems across Europe, which are used for both commercial purposes and irrigation, the *Canal de Castilla* will bring water from the mountains in the north of the *Meseta* and irrigate the cereal fields of Old Castile. ### Capituli de Corsica

| name | cor\_capituli\_di\_corsica |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Capituli de Corsica |
| description | We will formalize the *Capituli* — written agreements between the central Crown and the rugged mountain communes of the interior. These charters will grant the highlanders specific tax exemptions and the right to local self-governance in exchange for their perpetual military service and loyalty. This is a masterstroke of internal diplomacy that will trade royal authority for absolute loyalty, ensuring that the most difficult terrain on the island is held by a motivated, state-aligned citizenry rather than rebellious feudal lords. |

### Caravel

| name | unlock\_caravel\_advance |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Caravel |
| description | The caravel was a small, very maneuverable ship which could sail with high precision on long discovery journeys. Although designs varied, a caravel had a foresail, a square mainsail and a lateen mizzen. Its smaller size limited the number of guns onboard, but it also meant that this light ship could explore shallow coastal waters and estuaries. Vasco Da Gama, Cabot, Columbus and Magellan used caravels during their late 15th century and early 16th century voyages. |

### Cardinal's Bulwark

| name | spl\_cardinals\_bulwark |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Cardinal's Bulwark |
| description | Cardinal Albornoz built the *Rocca Albornoziana* not as a gesture but as a declaration — a fortress of such impregnability that every petty lord between Rome and Perugia understood that central power had returned in earnest, and that Spoleto stood as its most fortified instrument in central Italy. We have inherited both the structure and the institutional role it embodies, maintaining our city as the most orderly and disciplined administrative center in the region through every upheaval of the age. This combination of military solidity and civic order keeps our population unified, our burgher class literate in their civic duties, and our walls resistant to the storms of revolt and rebellion that trouble less well-governed territories. |

### Carnevale

| name | ven\_venetian\_thalassocracy |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Carnevale |
| description | In the weeks before Lent, the cities of our culture put on the *Bauta* and briefly become equals — a merchant in a mask walks beside a senator and cannot be distinguished, and the rules that govern dress, movement, and address are suspended by universal consent. This tradition of supervised disorder has served us for centuries as a pressure valve, and it has drawn visitors from across Europe who spend freely while marveling at the spectacle. This annual carnival sustains our reputation for a kind of governed liberty that no other culture in Europe can quite replicate. |

### Carrack

| name | unlock\_carrack\_advance |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Carrack |
| description | The design of the carrack, a late medieval ship, combined the features of Germanic longships and Arabic merchantmen. It was less maneuverable than the caravel, but because of its larger size, it could carry provisions for long voyages, cargo for trade, and guns for self-defense. Some carracks such as the English vessel 'Henri Grâce à Dieu' weighed more than 1,000 tons, had large fore and aft castles, and could be considered as the greatest warships of their time. |

### Carte Românească de Învățătură

| name | rmn\_cartea\_romaneasca\_de\_invetatura |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Carte Românească de Învățătură |
| description | We have issued the first printed collection of laws in our nation, the *Carte Românească de Învățătură*. This monumental work seeks to unify the legal practices of our realm and align them with broader European jurisprudence. By codifying Byzantine canon law into the vernacular and standardizing punishments, we have curbed the arbitrary power of the local boyars and bound the metropolitan courts to the throne, making our state a model of Eastern Orthodox legalism where the Prince's edicts carry the weight of both throne and altar. |

### Center of the Mediterranean

| name | maltese\_center\_of\_the\_mediterranean |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Center of the Mediterranean |
| description | As our island lies in the middle of the Mediterranean Sea, our maritime culture has shifted toward industrial ship-repair and global logistics. Our Grand Harbor will become the premier naval dockyard of the world as local artisans adapt their ancient stone-working and carpentry skills to the era of steam and iron. This industrial seamanship will turn the islands into a global coaling station and repair hub, ensuring that our fleet remains technologically superior to those of our rivals. |

### Centralize the States

| name | centralize\_the\_states |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Centralize the States |
| description | The Council of Trent has reformed doctrine and sharpened the institutional boundaries of the Church, but the Papal States themselves remain a patchwork of cities, lordships, and client territories whose local princes have long treated our authority as negotiable. We act now to close that gap — absorbing the revenues, appointments, and legal jurisdictions that local lords have quietly accumulated over generations, and placing them under direct administration from Rome in the manner that the reformed *Curia* demands. This consolidation of crown authority over our territories gives the Papacy the financial and administrative foundation that the Counter-Reformation's ambitions require. |

### Chjam'è Rispondi

| name | corsican\_chjame\_rispondi |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Chjam'è Rispondi |
| description | During the long years of foreign military pressure, the Corsican oral tradition of *Chjam'è Rispondi* (Call and Response) evolved into a sophisticated tool of social cohesion. This improvised poetic competition allowed the people to debate politics, honor their fallen heroes, and mock their occupiers through coded metaphors in the town squares. This all has ensured that the principles of the Enlightenment and the memories of the Revolution remain accessible to even the illiterate segments of society. By encouraging this we will create a unified cultural narrative that is immune to censorship, ensuring that the flame of Corsican identity remains burning bright through every situation. |

### Coastal Careening

| name | repair\_at\_sea |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Coastal Careening |
| description | Ships are undertaking longer voyages than ever before, made possible by new technical advances. This means more complex repairs are needed, but ports with adequate equipment and skilled craftsmen are not always available. Our sailors must be trained in the art of careening — deliberately beaching vessels to expose the parts that need cleaning or repair. This is the only way our fleets can maintain seaworthiness far from their home ports. |

| name | lat\_codex\_latinus\_advance |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Codex Latinus |
| description | The Emperor has ordered the complete overhaul of the legal system, merging the Justinian Code with the Frankish-style *Assizes of Romania* into a single, unified legal body. This *Codex Latinus* removes the legal distinctions between Frankish and Greek subjects, creating a single class of Imperial Citizens under the Emperor's law. This legal unification is the final nail in the coffin of the old feudal order, paving the way for a truly centralized, absolute monarchy. |

### Cog

| name | unlock\_cog\_advance |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Cog |
| description | The cog was a small ship widely used in medieval Europe. Characterized by having a single mast with a square sail, and constructed with overlapping planks, it was an evolution of the Viking merchant ships. Their construction made them more suitable for navigating the North Atlantic than the Mediterranean vessels, while their simpler rigging required fewer people to operate. It was used extensively by the Hanseatic League until eventually replaced by the hulk. |

### Communal Tradition

| name | ven\_venetian\_palaces |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Communal Tradition |
| description | The cities of the Veneto built their wealth and order on the *Comùn* (*Comune* in standard Italian: the Commune) — a covenant between burghers and nobility to share the obligations of governance rather than leave them to a single lord. From Padua to Verona and the mountain towns beyond, this tradition of elected councils, written statutes, and civic courts gave our urban life a stability and confidence that purely feudal regions could not match. This communal heritage makes our institutions resilient and our burghers invested in the success of the city they help govern. |
| icon | Advance republican foundation law advance.png |

### Comune Privileges

| name | dal\_comune\_privileges |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Comune Privileges |
| description | To secure the loyalty of the coastal cities against Venetian encroachment, we will issue privileges that guarantee the internal autonomy and Latin laws of those comunes (a unit of government). By allowing these cities to elect their own priors and manage their own local taxes, we create a loyal urban class that views us as a protector rather than an oppressor. This decentralized urban defense ensures that the coast is a patchwork of loyal, fortified comunes that can hold out against maritime raids, bringing the coasts and inner lands together. |

### Concentration of Power

| name | concentration\_power |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Concentration of Power |
| description | For too long, our nation has been unevenly governed by multiple *Stadtholders* across different provinces, with the most powerful acting as *Primus inter Pares* among their peers. It is time to make the position hereditary and concentrate authority in a single ruler. |

| --- | --- |

### Court of Great Refugees

| name | court\_of\_great\_refugees |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Court of Great Refugees |
| description | Dante Alighieri spent years of his exile walking our streets, and his presence drew others — poets, scholars, and master builders who found in Verona's Roman ruins and abundant stone a city still worth adding to. We extend this tradition of welcome into deliberate policy, offering patronage to displaced talent and drawing the finest craftsmen to the ancient workshops of the *Marmorina*, where the stone-cutting skills of generations meet the fresh techniques that newcomers bring from across Italy. This reputation for hospitality draws artists of unusual quality and gives our ambitious construction projects a depth of skilled labor that rivals who must import both talent and stone cannot easily match. |

### Cunning Diplomacy

| name | cunning\_diplomacy |
| --- | --- |
| display\_name | Cunning Diplomacy |
| description | Under the visionary leadership of our rulers, we will achieve the geopolitical impossible: the unification of all Romanian lands under a single banner. This 'Great Idea' will ignite a latent national consciousness that will transcend ancient feudal borders, proving to the world that a unified *Dacia* can stand as a European Great Power in its own right.
